<span class="atom-split" data-atom="macbeth" data-title="MACBETH" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro"></span>


[[characters/macbeth|MACBETH]]
BY WILLIAM SHAKESPEARE (ADAPTED BY THOMAS MIDDLETON)
SHORTLY after James VI of Scotland succeeded to the English throne, in 1603, he gave his patronage to Shakespeare’s company; the Lord Chamberlain’s Men became the [[archetypes/king|King]]’s Men, entering into a special relationship with their sovereign. [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] is the play of Shakespeare’s that most clearly reflects this relationship. James regarded the virtuous and noble [[characters/banquo|Banquo]], [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]’s comrade at the start of the action, as his direct ancestor; eight Stuart kings were said to have preceded James, just as, in the play, [[characters/banquo|Banquo]] points to ‘a show of eight kings’ as his descendants (4.1.127.1-140); and in the play the English [[archetypes/king|king]] (historically Edward the Confessor) is praised for the capacity, on which James also prided himself, to cure ’the [[archetypes/king|king]]’s evil’ (scrofula). [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] is obviously a Jacobean play, composed probably in 1606.
But the first printed text, in the 1623 Folio, shows signs of having been adapted at a later date. It is exceptionally short by comparison with Shakespeare’s other tragedies; and it includes episodes which there is good reason to believe are not by Shakespeare. Most conspicuous are Act 3, Scene 5 and parts of Act 4, Scene I: 38.I-60 and 141-8.1. These episodes feature Hecate, who does not appear elsewhere in the play; they are composed largely in octosyllabic couplets in a style conspicuously different from the rest of the play; and they call for the performance of two songs that are found in The [[characters/witch|Witch]], a play of uncertain date by Thomas Middleton. Probably Middleton himself adapted Shakespeare’s play some years after its first performance, adding these and more localized details, and cutting the play elsewhere. We do not attempt to excise passages most clearly written by Middleton, because the adapter’s hand almost certainly affected the text at other, less determinable points. The Folio text of [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] cites only the opening words of the songs; drawing on The [[characters/witch|Witch]], we attempt a reconstruction of their staging in [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]].
Shakespeare took materials for his story from the account in Raphael Holinshed’s Chronicle of the reigns of [[characters/duncan|Duncan]] and [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] (AD 1034-57). Occasionally (especially in the English episodes of Act 4, Scene 2) he closely followed Holinshed’s wording, but essentially the play’s structure is his own. He invented the framework of the [[characters/three-witches|three witches]] who tempted both [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] and [[characters/banquo|Banquo]] with prophecies of [[concepts/greatness|greatness]]. His [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] is both more introspective and more intensely evil than the competent warrior-[[archetypes/king|king]] portrayed by Holinshed; conversely, Shakespeare made [[characters/duncan|Duncan]], the [[archetypes/king|king]] whom [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] murders, far more venerable and saintly. Some of the play’s features, notably the character of [[characters/lady-macbeth|Lady Macbeth]], originate in Holinshed’s account of the murder of an earlier Scottish [[archetypes/king|king]], Duff; he was killed in his [[settings/castle|castle]] at Forres by Donwald, who had been ‘set on’ by his wife.
[[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] is an exciting story of witchcraft, murder, and retribution that can also be seen as a study in the philosophy and psychology of evil. The witches are not easily made credible in modern performances, and Shakespeare seems deliberately to have drained colour away from some parts of his composition in order to concentrate attention on [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]] and his Lady. It is [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]’s neurotic [[concepts/self|self]]-absorption, his [[concepts/fear|fear]], his anger, and his [[concepts/despair|despair]], along with his wife’s steely determination, her invoking of the powers of evil, and her eventual revelation in [[motifs/sleep|sleep]] of her repressed humanity, that have given the play its long-proven power to fascinate readers and to challenge performers.
THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY
[[archetypes/king|KING]] [[characters/duncan|DUNCAN]] of Scotland
A CAPTAIN in [[characters/duncan|Duncan]]’s army
[[characters/macbeth|MACBETH]], Thane of Glamis, later Thane of Cawdor, then [[archetypes/king|King]] of
Scotland
A PORTER at [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]’s [[settings/castle|castle]]
Three MURDERERS attending on [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]
SEYTON, servant of [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]
[[characters/lady-macbeth|LADY MACBETH]], [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]’s wife
[[characters/banquo|BANQUO]], a Scottish thane
FLEANCE, his son
[[characters/macduff|MACDUFF]], Thane of Fife
[[characters/lady-macduff|LADY MACDUFF]], his wife
[[characters/macduff|MACDUFF]]’S SON
SIWARD, [[characters/earl-of-northumberland|Earl of Northumberland]]
YOUNG SIWARD, his son
An English DOCTOR

HECATE, [[characters/queen|Queen]] of the Witches
Six WITCHES
Three APPARITIONS, one an armed head, one a bloody [[archetypes/child|child]], one
a [[archetypes/child|child]] crowned
A SPIRIT LIKE A CAT
Other SPIRITS

An OLD MAN
A MESSENGER
MURDERERS
SERVANTS

A show of eight kings; Lords and Thanes, attendants, soldiers, drummers

The [[forms/tragedy|Tragedy]] of [[characters/macbeth|Macbeth]]

### Atto 1, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Thunder and [[motifs/lightning\|lightning]]. Enter [[characters/three-witches\|three Witches]] |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | When shall we three meet again?<br>In thunder, [[motifs/lightning\|lightning]], or in rain? |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | When the hurly-burly’s done,<br>When the battle’s lost and won. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | That will be ere the set of sun. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Where the place? |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Upon the heath. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | There to meet with [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | I come, Grimalkin. |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Paddock calls. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Anon. |
| ALL | Fair is foul, and foul is fair,<br>Hover through the fog and filthy air. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum within. Enter [[archetypes/king\|king]] [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]], [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], Donalbain, Lennox, with attendants, meeting a bleeding Captain |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | What bloody man is that? He can report,<br>As seemeth by his plight, of the revolt<br>The newest state. |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | This is the sergeant<br>Who like a good and hardy soldier fought<br>’Gainst my captivity. Hail, brave friend.<br>Say to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] the knowledge of the broil<br>As thou didst leave it. |
| CAPTAIN | Doubtful it stood,<br>As two spent swimmers that do cling together<br>And choke their art. The merciless Macdonald—<br>Worthy to be a [[archetypes/rebel\|rebel]], for to that<br>The multiplying villainies of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Do swarm upon him—from the Western Isles<br>Of kerns and galloglasses is supplied,<br>And fortune on his damned quarry smiling<br>Showed like a [[archetypes/rebel\|rebel]]’s whore. But all’s too weak,<br>For brave [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]—[[motifs/well\|well]] he deserves that name!—<br>Disdaining fortune, with his brandished steel<br>Which smoked with bloody execution,<br>Like valour’s minion<br>Carved out his passage till he faced the slave,<br>Which ne’er shook hands nor bade farewell to him<br>Till he unseamed him from the nave to th’ chops,<br>And fixed his head upon our battlements. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | O valiant cousin, worthy gentleman! |
| CAPTAIN | As whence the sun ’gins his reflection<br>Shipwrecking storms and direful thunders break,<br>So from that [[motifs/spring\|spring]] whence comfort seemed to come<br>Discomfort swells. Mark, [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Scotland, mark.<br>No sooner justice had, with valour armed,<br>Compelled these skipping kerns to trust their heels<br>But the Norwegian lord, surveying vantage,<br>With furbished arms and new supplies of men<br>Began a fresh assault. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Dismayed not this our captains, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] and<br>[[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]? |
| CAPTAIN | Yes, as sparrows eagles, or the hare the [[motifs/lion\|lion]]!<br>If I say sooth I must report they were<br>As cannons overcharged with [[topoi/double\|double]] cracks,<br>So they doubly redoubled strokes upon the foe.<br>Except they meant to bathe in reeking wounds<br>Or memorize another Golgotha,<br>I cannot tell—<br>But I am faint. My gashes cry for help. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | So [[motifs/well\|well]] thy words become thee as thy wounds:<br>They smack of [[concepts/honour\|honour]] both.—Go get him surgeons. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Captain with attendants |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Ross and Angus |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Who comes here? |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | The worthy Thane of Ross. |
| LENNOX | What haste looks through his eyes! So should he look<br>That seems to speak things strange.<br>Ross<br>God save the [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Whence cam’st thou, worthy thane?<br>Ross<br>From Fife, great [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>Where the Norwegian banners flout the sky<br>And fan our people cold.<br>Norway himself, with terrible numbers,<br>Assisted by that most disloyal traitor<br>The Thane of Cawdor, began a dismal conflict,<br>Till that Bellona’s bridegroom, lapped in proof,<br>Confronted him with [[concepts/self\|self]]-comparisons,<br>Point against point, rebellious arm ’gainst arm,<br>Curbing his lavish spirit; and to conclude,<br>The victory fell on us— |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Great happiness.<br>Ross<br>That now<br>Sweno, the Norways’ [[archetypes/king\|king]], craves composition;<br>Nor would we deign him burial of his men<br>Till he disbursed at Saint Colum’s inch<br>Ten thousand dollars to our general use. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | No more that Thane of Cawdor shall deceive<br>Our bosom interest. Go pronounce his present [[motifs/death\|death]],<br>And with his former title greet [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]].<br>Ross I’ll see it done. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | What he hath lost, noble [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] hath won. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 1, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Thunder. Enter the [[characters/three-witches\|three Witches]] |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Where hast thou been, sister? |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Killing swine. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Sister, where thou? |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | A sailor’s wife had chestnuts in her lap,<br>And munched, and munched, and munched. ‘Give<br>me,’ quoth I.<br>‘Aroint thee, [[characters/witch\|witch]],’ the rump-fed runnion cries.<br>Her husband’s to Aleppo gone, master o’th’ Tiger.<br>But in a sieve I’ll thither sail,<br>And like a rat without a tail<br>I’ll do, I’ll do, and I’ll do. |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | I’ll give thee a [[motifs/wind\|wind]]. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Thou’rt kind. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | And I another. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | I myself have all the other,<br>And the very ports they blow,<br>All the quarters that they know<br>I’th’ shipman’s card.<br>I’ll drain him dry as hay.<br>[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] shall neither [[motifs/night\|night]] nor day<br>Hang upon his penthouse lid.<br>He shall live a man forbid.<br>Weary sennights nine times nine<br>Shall he dwindle, peak, and pine.<br>Though his barque cannot be lost,<br>Yet it shall be tempest-tossed.<br>Look what I have. |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Show me, show me. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Here I have a pilot’s thumb,<br>Wrecked as homeward he did come. |
| *(didascalia)* | Drum within |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | A drum, a drum—<br>[[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] doth come. |
| ALL | (dancing in a ring)<br>The weird sisters hand in hand,<br>Posters of the sea and land,<br>Thus do go about, about,<br>Thrice to thine, and thrice to mine,<br>And thrice again to make up nine.<br>Peace! The charm’s wound up. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] and [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | So foul and fair a day I have not seen. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | How far is’t called to Forres?—What are these,<br>So withered, and so wild in their attire,<br>That look not like th‘inhabitants o’th’ earth<br>And yet are on’t?—Live you, or are you aught<br>That man may question? You seem to understand me<br>By each at once her choppy finger laying<br>Upon her skinny lips. You should be women,<br>And yet your beards forbid me to interpret<br>That you are so. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (to the Witches)<br>Speak, if you can. What are you? |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | All hail, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]! Hail to thee, Thane of Glamis. |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | All hail, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]! Hail to thee, Thane of Cawdor. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | All hail, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], that shalt be [[archetypes/king\|king]] hereafter! |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Good sir, why do you start and seem to [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Things that do sound so fair? (To the Witches) I’th’<br>name of [[concepts/truth\|truth]],<br>Are ye fantastical or that indeed<br>Which outwardly ye show? My noble partner<br>You greet with present grace and great prediction<br>Of noble having and of royal [[concepts/hope\|hope]],<br>That he seems rapt withal. To me you speak not.<br>If you can look into the seeds of [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>And say which grain will grow and which will not,<br>Speak then to me, who neither beg nor [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Your favours nor your hate. |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Hail! |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Hail! |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Hail! |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Lesser than [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], and greater. |
| SECOND [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Not so happy, yet much happier. |
| THIRD [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | Thou shalt get kings, though thou be none.<br>So all hail, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] and [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]! |
| FIRST [[characters/witch\|WITCH]] | [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], all hail! |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Stay, you imperfect speakers, tell me more.<br>By Sinel’s [[motifs/death\|death]] I know I am Thane of Glamis,<br>But how of Cawdor? The Thane of Cawdor lives,<br>A prosperous gentleman, and to be [[archetypes/king\|king]]<br>Stands not within the prospect of belief,<br>No more than to be Cawdor. Say from whence<br>You owe this strange intelligence, or why<br>Upon this blasted heath you stop our way<br>With such prophetic greeting. Speak, I charge you.<br>The Witches vanish |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | The earth hath bubbles, as the [[motifs/water\|water]] has,<br>And these are of them. Whither are they vanished? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Into the air, and what seemed corporal<br>Melted as breath into the [[motifs/wind\|wind]]. Would they had stayed. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Were such things here as we do speak about,<br>Or have we eaten on the insane root<br>That takes the reason prisoner? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Your children shall be kings. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | You shall be [[archetypes/king\|king]]. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | And Thane of Cawdor too. Went it not so? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | To th’ [[concepts/self\|self]]-same tune and words. Who’s here? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Ross and Angus |
| ROSS | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] hath happily received, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]],<br>The news of thy success, and when he reads<br>Thy personal venture in the rebels’ sight<br>His wonders and his praises do contend<br>Which should be thine or his; silenced with that,<br>In viewing o‘er the rest o’th’ [[concepts/self\|self]]-same day<br>He finds thee in the stout Norwegian ranks,<br>Nothing afeard of what thyself didst make,<br>Strange images of [[motifs/death\|death]]. As thick as hail<br>Came post with post, and every one did bear<br>Thy praises in his kingdom’s great defence,<br>And poured them down before him. |
| ANGUS | (to [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]])<br>We are sent<br>To give thee from our royal master thanks;<br>Only to herald thee into his sight,<br>Not pay thee. |
| ROSS | And, for an earnest of a greater [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>He bade me from him call thee Thane of Cawdor,<br>In which addition, hail, most worthy thane,<br>For it is thine. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | What, can the devil speak true? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | The Thane of Cawdor lives. Why do you dress me<br>In borrowed robes? |
| ANGUS | Who was the thane lives yet,<br>But under heavy judgement bears that life<br>Which he deserves to lose. Whether he was combined<br>With those of Norway, or did line the [[archetypes/rebel\|rebel]]<br>With hidden help and vantage, or that with both<br>He laboured in his country’s wrack, I know not;<br>But treasons capital, confessed, and proved<br>Have overthrown him. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (aside)<br>Glamis, and Thane of Cawdor.<br>The greatest is behind. (To Ross and Angus) Thanks for<br>your pains.<br>(To [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]) Do you not [[concepts/hope\|hope]] your children shall be kings<br>When those that gave the thane of Cawdor to me<br>Promised no less to them? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | That, trusted [[motifs/home\|home]],<br>Might yet enkindle you unto the [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>Besides the thane of Cawdor. But ’tis strange,<br>And oftentimes to win us to our harm<br>The instruments of darkness tell us truths,<br>Win us with honest trifles to betray’s<br>In deepest consequence.<br>(To Ross and Angus) Cousins, a word, I pray you. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (aside) Two truths are told<br>As happy prologues to the swelling act<br>Of the imperial theme. (To Ross and Angus) I thank<br>you, gentlemen.<br>(Aside) This [[motifs/supernatural\|supernatural]] soliciting<br>Cannot be ill, cannot be good. If ill,<br>Why hath it given me earnest of success<br>Commencing in a [[concepts/truth\|truth]]? I am Thane of Cawdor.<br>If good, why do I yield to that suggestion<br>Whose horrid image doth unfix my hair<br>And make my seated heart knock at my ribs<br>Against the use of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]? Present fears<br>Are less than horrible imaginings.<br>My thought, whose murder yet is but fantastical,<br>Shakes so my single state of man that function<br>Is smothered in surmise, and nothing is<br>But what is not. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | (to Ross and Angus)<br>Look how our partner’s rapt.<br>[[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]](aside)<br>If chance will have me [[archetypes/king\|king]], why, chance may [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>me<br>Without my stir. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | (to Ross and Angus)<br>New honours come upon him,<br>Like our strange garments, cleave not to their mould<br>But with the aid of use. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (aside)<br>Come what come may,<br>[[motifs/time\|Time]] and the hour runs through the roughest day. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Worthy [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], we stay upon your leisure. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Give me your favour. My dull brain was wrought<br>With things forgotten. (To Ross and Angus) Kind<br>gentlemen, your pains<br>Are registered where every day I turn<br>The leaf to read them. Let us toward the [[archetypes/king\|King]].<br>(Aside to [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]) Think upon what hath chanced, and<br>at more [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>The interim having weighed it, let us speak<br>Our free hearts each to other. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Very gladly. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Till then, enough. (To Ross and Angus) Come, friends. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]], Lennox, [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], Donalbain, and attendants |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Is execution done on Cawdor? Are not<br>Those in commission yet returned? |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | My liege,<br>They are not yet come back. But I have spoke<br>With one that saw him die, who did report<br>That very frankly he confessed his treasons,<br>Implored your highness’ pardon, and set forth<br>A deep repentance. Nothing in his life<br>Became him like the leaving it. He died<br>As one that had been studied in his [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>To throw away the dearest thing he owed<br>As ’twere a careless trifle. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | There’s no art<br>To find the mind’s construction in the face.<br>He was a gentleman on whom I built<br>An absolute trust. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], Ross, and Angus |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | (To [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]) O worthiest cousin,<br>The sin of my ingratitude even now<br>Was heavy on me! Thou art so far before<br>That swiftest wing of recompense is slow<br>To overtake thee. Would thou hadst less deserved,<br>That the proportion both of thanks and payment<br>Might have been mine. Only I have left to say,<br>‘More is thy due than more than all can pay’. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | The service and the loyalty I owe,<br>In doing it, pays itself. Your highness’ part<br>Is to receive our duties, and our duties<br>Are to your throne and state children and servants<br>Which do but what they should by doing everything<br>Safe toward your [[motifs/love\|love]] and [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Welcome hither.<br>I have begun to plant thee, and will labour<br>To make thee full of growing.—NoHe [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]],<br>That hast no less deserved, nor must be known<br>No less to have done so, let me enfold thee<br>And hold thee to my heart. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | There if I grow<br>The harvest is your own. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | My plenteous joys,<br>Wanton in fullness, seek to hide themselves<br>In drops of sorrow. Sons, kinsmen, thanes,<br>And you whose places are the nearest, know<br>We will establish our estate upon<br>Our eldest, [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], whom we name hereafter<br>The Prince of Cumberland; which [[concepts/honour\|honour]] must<br>Not unaccompanied invest him only,<br>But signs of nobleness, like [[motifs/stars\|stars]], shall shine<br>On all deservers. (To [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]) From hence to Inverness,<br>And bind us further to you. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | The rest is labour which is not used for you.<br>I’ll be myself the harbinger, and make joyful<br>The hearing of my wife with your approach;<br>So humbly take my leave. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | My worthy Cawdor. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (aside)<br>The Prince of Cumberland—that is a step<br>On which I must fall down or else o’erleap,<br>For in my way it lies. [[motifs/stars\|Stars]], hide your fires,<br>Let not light see my black and deep desires;<br>The eye wink at the hand; yet let that be<br>Which the eye fears, when it is done, to see. Exit |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | True, worthy [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], he is full so valiant,<br>And in his commendations I am fed.<br>It is a banquet to me. Let’s after him,<br>Whose care is gone before to bid us welcome.<br>It is a peerless kinsman. Flourish. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]], with a letter |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | (reading) ‘They met me in the day of success, and I have learned by the perfect’st report they have more in them than mortal knowledge. When I burned in [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to question them further, they made themselves air, into which they vanished. Whiles I stood rapt in the [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] of it came missives from the [[archetypes/king\|King]], who all-hailed me “Thane of Cawdor”, by which title before these weird sisters saluted me, and referred me to the coming on of [[motifs/time\|time]] with “Hail, [[archetypes/king\|King]] that shalt be!” This have I thought good to deliver thee, my dearest partner of [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]], that thou mightst not lose the dues of rejoicing by being ignorant of what [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] is promised thee. Lay it to thy heart, and farewell.’<br>Glamis thou art, and Cawdor, and shalt be<br>What thou art promised. Yet do I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] thy [[concepts/nature\|nature]].<br>It is too full o‘th’ milk of human kindness<br>To catch the nearest way. Thou wouldst be great,<br>Art not without [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]], but without<br>The illness should attend it. What thou wouldst highly,<br>That wouldst thou holily; wouldst not play false,<br>And yet wouldst wrongly win. Thou’dst have, great<br>Glamis,<br>That which cries ‘Thus thou must do’ if thou have it,<br>And that which rather thou dost [[concepts/fear\|fear]] to do<br>Than wishest should be undone. Hie thee hither,<br>That I may pour my spirits in thine ear<br>And chastise with the valour of my tongue<br>All that impedes thee from the golden round<br>Which fate and metaphysical aid doth seem<br>To have thee crowned withal. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Servant |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | What is your tidings? |
| SERVANT | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] comes here tonight. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Thou’t mad to say it.<br>Is not thy master with him, who, were’t so,<br>Would have informed for preparation? |
| SERVANT | So please you, it is true. Our thane is coming,<br>One of my fellows had the [[characters/speed\|speed]] of him,<br>Who, almost dead for breath, had scarcely more<br>Than would make up his message. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Give him tending;<br>He brings great news. Exit Servant<br>The raven himself is hoarse<br>That croaks the fatal entrance of [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]<br>Under my battlements. Come, you spirits<br>That tend on mortal thoughts, unsex me here,<br>And fill me from the [[motifs/crown\|crown]] to the toe top-full<br>Of direst cruelty. Make thick my [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>Stop up th‘access and passage to remorse,<br>That no compunctious visitings of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Shake my fell purpose, nor keep peace between<br>Th’effect and it. Come to my woman’s breasts,<br>And take my milk for gall, you murd‘ring ministers,<br>Wherever in your sightless substances<br>You wait on [[concepts/nature\|nature]]’s mischief. Come, thick [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>And pall thee in the dunnest smoke of hell,<br>That my keen knife see not the wound it makes,<br>Nor heaven peep through the blanket of the dark<br>To cry ’Hold, hold!’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Great Glamis, worthy Cawdor,<br>Greater than both by the all-hail hereafter,<br>Thy letters have transported me beyond<br>This ignorant present, and I feel now<br>The future in the instant. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | My dearest [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>[[characters/duncan\|Duncan]] comes here tonight. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | And when goes hence? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Tomorrow, as he purposes. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | O never<br>Shall sun that morrow see.<br>Your face, my thane, is as a book where men<br>May read strange matters. To beguile the [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>Look like the [[motifs/time\|time]]; bear welcome in your eye,<br>Your hand, your tongue; look like the [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] flower,<br>But be the serpent under’t. He that’s coming<br>Must be provided for; and you shall put<br>This [[motifs/night\|night]]’s great business into my dispatch,<br>Which shall to all our nights and days to come<br>Give solely sovereign sway and masterdom. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | We will speak further. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Only look up clear.<br>To alter favour ever is to [[concepts/fear\|fear]].<br>Leave all the rest to me. Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 1.6 Hautboys and torches. Enter [[archetypes/king\|King]] [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]], [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], Donalbain, [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], Lennox, [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]], Ross, Angus, and attendants |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | This [[settings/castle\|castle]] hath a pleasant seat. The air<br>Nimbly and sweetly recommends itself<br>Unto our gentle senses. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | This guest of [[motifs/summer\|summer]],<br>The temple-haunting martlet, does approve<br>By his loved mansionry that the heavens’ breath<br>Smells wooingly here. No jutty, frieze,<br>Buttress, nor coign of vantage but this bird<br>Hath made his pendant bed and procreant cradle;<br>Where they most breed and haunt I have observed<br>The air is delicate. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | See, see, our honoured hostess!<br>The [[motifs/love\|love]] that follows us sometime is our trouble,<br>Which still we thank as [[motifs/love\|love]]. Herein I teach you<br>How you shall bid God ’ield us for your pains,<br>And thank us for your trouble. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | All our service<br>In every point twice done, and then done [[topoi/double\|double]],<br>Were poor and single business to contend<br>Against those honours deep and broad wherewith<br>Your majesty loads our house. For those of old,<br>And the late dignities heaped up to them,<br>We rest your hermits. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Where’s the Thane of Cawdor?<br>We coursed him at the heels, and had a purpose<br>To be his purveyor; but he rides [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>And his great [[motifs/love\|love]], sharp as his spur, hath holp him<br>To his [[motifs/home\|home]] before us. Fair and noble hostess,<br>We are your guest tonight. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Your servants ever<br>Have theirs, themselves, and what is theirs in count<br>To make their audit at your highness’ pleasure,<br>Still to return your own. |
| [[archetypes/king\|KING]] [[characters/duncan\|DUNCAN]] | Give me your hand.<br>Conduct me to mine host. We [[motifs/love\|love]] him highly,<br>And shall continue our graces towards him.<br>By your leave, hostess. Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Hautboys. Torches. Enter a sewer and divers servants with dishes and service over the stage. Then enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | If it were done when ‘tis done, then ’twere [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>It were done quickly. If th‘assassination<br>Could trammel up the consequence, and catch<br>With his surcease success: that but this blow<br>Might be the be-all and the end-all, here,<br>But here upon this bank and shoal of [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>We’d jump the life to come. But in these cases<br>We still have judgement here, that we but teach<br>Bloody instructions which, being taught, return<br>To plague th’inventor. This even-handed justice<br>Commends th‘ingredience of our poisoned chalice<br>To our own lips. He’s here in [[topoi/double\|double]] trust:<br>First, as I am his kinsman and his subject,<br>Strong both against the deed; then, as his host,<br>Who should against his murderer shut the door,<br>Not bear the knife myself. Besides, this [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]<br>Hath borne his faculties so meek, hath been<br>So clear in his great office, that his virtues<br>Will plead like angels, trumpet-tongued against<br>The deep damnation of his taking-off,<br>And pity, like a naked new-born babe,<br>Striding the blast, or heaven’s cherubin, horsed<br>Upon the sightless couriers of the air,<br>Shall blow the horrid deed in every eye<br>That [[motifs/tears\|tears]] shall drown the [[motifs/wind\|wind]]. I have no spur<br>To prick the sides of my intent, but only<br>Vaulting [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]] which o’erleaps itself<br>And falls on th’other. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | How now? What news? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | He has almost supped. Why have you left the<br>chamber? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Hath he asked for me? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Know you not he has? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | We will proceed no further in this business.<br>He hath honoured me of late, and I have bought<br>Golden opinions from all sorts of people,<br>Which would be worn now in their newest gloss,<br>Not cast aside so soon. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Was the [[concepts/hope\|hope]] drunk<br>Wherein you dressed yourself? Hath it slept since?<br>And wakes it now to look so green and pale<br>At what it did so freely? From this [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Such I account thy [[motifs/love\|love]]. Art thou afeard<br>To be the same in thine own act and valour<br>As thou art in [[motifs/desire\|desire]]? Wouldst thou have that<br>Which thou esteem‘st the ornament of life,<br>And live a coward in thine own esteem,<br>Letting ’I dare not’ wait upon ‘I would’,<br>Like the poor cat i’th’ adage? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Prithee, peace.<br>I dare do all that may become a man;<br>Who dares do more is none. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | What beast was’t then<br>That made you break this enterprise to me?<br>When you durst do it, then you were a man;<br>And to be more than what you were, you would<br>Be so much more the man. Nor [[motifs/time\|time]] nor place<br>Did then adhere, and yet you would make both.<br>They have made themselves, and that their fitness now<br>Does unmake you. I have given suck, and know<br>How tender ’tis to [[motifs/love\|love]] the babe that milks me.<br>I would, while it was smiling in my face,<br>Have plucked my nipple from his boneless gums<br>And dashed the brains out, had I so sworn<br>As you have done to this. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | If we should fail? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | We fail!<br>But screw your [[concepts/courage\|courage]] to the sticking-place<br>And we’ll not fail. When [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]] is asleep—<br>Whereto the rather shall his day’s hard [[motifs/journey\|journey]]<br>Soundly invite him—his two chamberlains<br>Will I with wine and wassail so convince<br>That [[motifs/memory\|memory]], the warder of the brain,<br>Shall be a fume, and the receipt of reason<br>A limbeck only. When in swinish [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]<br>Their drenched natures lies as in a [[motifs/death\|death]],<br>What cannot you and I perform upon<br>Th’unguarded [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]? What not put upon<br>His spongy officers, who shall bear the [[concepts/guilt\|guilt]]<br>Of our great quell? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Bring forth men-children only,<br>For thy undaunted mettle should compose<br>Nothing but males. Will it not be received,<br>When we have marked with [[motifs/blood\|blood]] those sleepy two<br>Of his own chamber and used their very daggers,<br>That they have done’t? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Who dares receive it other,<br>As we shall make our griefs and clamour roar<br>Upon his [[motifs/death\|death]]? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I am settled, and bend up<br>Each corporal agent to this terrible feat.<br>Away, and mock the [[motifs/time\|time]] with fairest show.<br>False face must hide what the false heart doth know. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and Fleance, with a torch before him |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | How goes the [[motifs/night\|night]], boy? |
| FLEANCE | The [[motifs/moon\|moon]] is down. I have not heard the [[motifs/clock\|clock]]. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | And she goes down at twelve. |
| FLEANCE | I take’t ’tis later, sir. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | (giving Fleance his sword)<br>Hold, take my sword. There’s husbandry in heaven,<br><br>Their candles are all out. Take thee that, too.<br>A heavy summons lies like lead upon me,<br>And yet I would not [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. Merciful powers,<br>Restrain in me the cursed thoughts that [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Gives way to in repose. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], and a servant with a torch |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Give me my sword. Who’s there? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | A friend. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | What, sir, not yet at rest? The [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s a-bed.<br>He hath been in unusual pleasure, and<br>Sent forth great largesse to your offices.<br>This diamond he greets your wife withal<br>By the name of most kind hostess, and shut up<br>In measureless content. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Being unprepared<br>Our will became the servant to defect,<br>Which else should free have wrought. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | All’s [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>I dreamt last [[motifs/night\|night]] of the three weird sisters.<br>To you they have showed some [[concepts/truth\|truth]]. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I think not of them;<br>Yet, when we can entreat an hour to serve,<br>We would spend it in some words upon that business<br>If you would grant the [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | At your kind’st leisure. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | If you shall cleave to my consent when ’tis,<br>It shall make [[concepts/honour\|honour]] for you. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | So I lose none<br>In seeking to augment it, but still keep<br>My bosom franchised and allegiance clear,<br>I shall be counselled. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Good repose the while. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Thanks, sir. The like to you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and Fleance |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (to the Servant)<br>Go bid thy mistress, when my drink is ready,<br>She strike upon the bell. Get thee to bed. Exit Servant<br>Is this a dagger which I see before me,<br>The handle toward my hand? Come, let me clutch thee.<br>I have thee not, and yet I see thee still.<br>Art thou not, fatal vision, sensible<br>To feeling as to sight? Or art thou but<br>A dagger of the mind, a false creation<br>Proceeding from the heat-oppressed brain?<br>I see thee yet, in form as palpable<br>As this which now I draw.<br>Thou marshall‘st me the way that I was going,<br>And such an instrument I was to use.<br>Mine eyes are made the fools o’th’ other senses,<br>Or else worth all the rest. I see thee still,<br>And on thy blade and dudgeon gouts of [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>Which was not so before. There’s no such thing.<br>It is the bloody business which informs<br>Thus to mine eyes. Now o’er the one half-world<br>[[concepts/nature\|Nature]] seems dead, and wicked [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]] abuse<br>The curtained [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. Witchcraft celebrates<br>Pale Hecate’s offerings, and withered murder,<br>Alarumed by his sentinel the wolf,<br>Whose howl’s his watch, thus with his stealthy pace,<br>With [[characters/tarquin\|Tarquin]]’s ravishing strides, towards his design<br>Moves like a [[archetypes/ghost\|ghost]]. Thou sure and firm-set earth,<br>Hear not my steps which way they walk, for [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Thy very stones prate of my whereabout,<br>And take the present horror from the [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>Which now suits with it. Whiles I threat, he lives.<br>Words to the heat of deeds too cold breath gives.<br>A bell rings<br><br>I go, and it is done. The bell invites me.<br>Hear it not, [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]; for it is a knell<br>That summons thee to heaven or to hell. Exit |

### Atto 2, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | That which hath made them drunk hath made me bold.<br>What hath quenched them hath given me [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. Hark,<br>peace!—<br>It was the owl that shrieked, the fatal bellman<br>Which gives the stern’st good-[[motifs/night\|night]]. He is about it.<br>The doors are open, and the surfeited grooms<br>Do mock their charge with snores. I have drugged<br>their possets<br>That [[motifs/death\|death]] and [[concepts/nature\|nature]] do contend about them<br>Whether they live or die. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] [above] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Who’s there?<br>What ho? Exit |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Alack, I am afraid they have awaked,<br>And ‘tis not done. Th’attempt and not the deed<br>Confounds us. Hark!—I laid their daggers ready;<br>He could not miss ’em. Had he not resembled<br>My father as he slept, I had done’t.<br>[Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] below]<br>My husband! |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I have done the deed. Didst thou not hear a noise? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | I heard the owl scream and the crickets cry.<br>Did not you speak? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | When? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Now. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | As I descended? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Ay. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Hark!—Who lies i’th’ second chamber? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Donalbain. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (looking at his hands) This is a sorry sight. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | A foolish thought, to say a sorry sight. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | There’s one did laugh in’s [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], and one cried ‘Murder!’<br>That they did wake each other. I stood and heard<br>them.<br>But they did say their prayers and addressed them<br>Again to [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | There are two lodged together. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | One cried ‘God bless us’ and ‘Amen’ the other,<br>As they had seen me with these hangman’s hands.<br>List‘ning their [[concepts/fear\|fear]] I could not say ‘Amen’<br>When they did say ‘God bless us.’ |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Consider it not so deeply. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | But wherefore could not I pronounce ‘Amen’?<br>I had most need of blessing, and ‘Amen’<br>Stuck in my throat. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | These deeds must not be thought<br>After these ways. So, it will make us mad. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Methought I heard a voice cry ‘[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] no more,<br>[[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] does murder [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]’—the [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]],<br>[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] that knits up the ravelled sleave of care,<br>The [[motifs/death\|death]] of each day’s life, sore labour’s bath,<br>Balm of hurt minds, great [[concepts/nature\|nature]]’s second course,<br>Chief nourisher in life’s feast— |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | What do you mean? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Still it cried ‘[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] no more’ to all the house,<br>‘Glamis hath murdered [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], and therefore Cawdor<br>Shall [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] no more, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] shall [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] no more.’ |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Who was it that thus cried? Why, worthy thane,<br>You do unbend your noble strength to think<br>So brain-sickly of things. Go get some [[motifs/water\|water]]<br>And wash this filthy witness from your hand.<br>Why did you bring these daggers from the place?<br>They must lie there. Go, carry them, and smear<br>The sleepy grooms with [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I’ll go no more.<br>I am afraid to think what I have done,<br>Look on’t again I dare not. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Infirm of purpose!<br>Give me the daggers. The sleeping and the dead<br>Are but as pictures. ’Tis the eye of [[motifs/childhood\|childhood]]<br>That fears a painted devil. If he do bleed<br>I’ll gild the faces of the grooms withal,<br>For it must seem their [[concepts/guilt\|guilt]]. Exit<br>Knock within |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Whence is that knocking?—<br>How is’t with me when every noise appals me?<br>What hands are here! Ha, they pluck out mine eyes.<br>Will all great Neptune’s ocean wash this [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>Clean from my hand? No, this my hand will rather<br>The multitudinous seas incarnadine,<br>Making the green one red. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | My hands are of your colour, but I shame<br>To wear a heart so white.<br>Knock within<br><br>I hear a knocking<br>At the south entry. Retire we to our chamber.<br>A little [[motifs/water\|water]] clears us of this deed.<br>How easy is it then! Your constancy<br>Hath left you unattended.<br>Knock within<br><br>Hark, more knocking.<br>Get on your nightgown, lest occasion call us<br>And show us to be watchers. Be not lost<br>So poorly in your thoughts. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | To know my deed ’twere best not know myself.<br>Knock within<br><br>Wake [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]] with thy knocking. I would thou<br>couldst. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Porter. Knocking within |
| PORTER | Here’s a knocking indeed! If a man were porter<br>of hell-gate he should have old turning the key.<br>Knock within<br><br>Knock, knock, knock. Who’s there, i‘th’ name of<br>Beelzebub? Here’s a farmer that hanged himself on<br>th’expectation of plenty. Come in [[motifs/time\|time]]! Have napkins<br>enough about you; here you’ll sweat for’t.<br>Knock within<br><br>Knock, knock. Who’s there, in th‘other devil’s name?<br>[[concepts/faith\|Faith]], here’s an equivocator that could swear in both<br>the scales against either scale, who committed treason<br>enough for God’s sake, yet could not equivocate to<br>heaven. O, come in, equivocator.<br>Knock within<br><br>Knock, knock, knock. Who’s there? ’[[concepts/faith\|Faith]], here’s an<br>English tailor come hither for stealing out of a French<br>hose. Come in, tailor. Here you may roast your goose.<br>Knock within<br><br>Knock, knock. Never at quiet. What are you?—But this<br>place is too cold for hell. I’ll devil-porter it no further.<br>I had thought to have let in some of all professions<br>that go the primrose way to th’everlasting bonfire.<br>Knock within<br><br>Anon, anon!<br>He opens the gate<br><br>I pray you remember the porter. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]] and Lennox |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Was it so late, friend, ere you went to bed<br>That you do lie so late? |
| PORTER | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], sir, we were carousing till the second cock, and drink, sir, is a great provoker of three things. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | What three things does drink especially provoke? |
| PORTER | Marry, sir, nose-painting, [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], and urine. Lechery, sir, it provokes and unprovokes: it provokes the [[motifs/desire\|desire]] but it takes away the performance. Therefore much drink may be said to be an equivocator with lechery: it makes him and it mars him; it sets him on and it takes him off; it persuades him and disheartens him, makes him stand to and not stand to; in conclusion, equivocates him in a [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], and, giving him the lie, [[motifs/leaves\|leaves]] him. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | I believe drink gave thee the lie last [[motifs/night\|night]]. |
| PORTER | That it did, sir, i’the very throat on me; but I requited him for his lie, and, I think, being too strong for him, though he took up my legs sometime, yet I made a shift to cast him. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Is thy master stirring? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Our knocking has awaked him: here he comes. [Exit Porter] |
| LENNOX | (to [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]])<br>Good morrow, noble sir. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Good morrow, both. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Is the [[archetypes/king\|King]] stirring, worthy thane? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Not yet. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | He did command me to call timely on him.<br>I have almost slipped the hour. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I’ll bring you to him. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | I know this is a joyful trouble to you,<br>But yet ’tis one. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | The labour we delight in physics [[motifs/pain\|pain]].<br>This is the door. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | I’ll make so bold to call,<br>For ’tis my limited service. Exit [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]] |
| LENNOX | Goes the [[archetypes/king\|King]] hence today? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | He does; he did appoint so. |
| LENNOX | The [[motifs/night\|night]] has been unruly. Where we lay<br>Our chimneys were blown down, and, as they say,<br>Lamentings heard i’th’ air, strange screams of [[motifs/death\|death]],<br>And prophesying with accents terrible<br>Of dire combustion and confused events<br>New-hatched to th’ woeful [[motifs/time\|time]]. The obscure bird<br>Clamoured the livelong [[motifs/night\|night]]. Some say the earth<br>Was feverous and did shake. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | ’Twas a rough [[motifs/night\|night]]. |
| LENNOX | My young remembrance cannot parallel<br>A fellow to it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]] |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | O horror, horror, horror!<br>Tongue nor heart cannot conceive nor name thee. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | and LENNOX What’s the matter? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Confusion now hath made his masterpiece.<br>Most sacrilegious murder hath broke ope<br>The Lord’s anointed temple and stole thence<br>The life o’th’ building. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | What is’t you say—the life? |
| LENNOX | Mean you his majesty? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Approach the chamber and destroy your sight<br>With a new Gorgon. Do not bid me speak.<br>See, and then speak yourselves. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] and Lennox |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Awake, awake!<br>Ring the alarum bell. Murder and treason!<br>[[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and Donalbain, [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], awake!<br>Shake off this downy [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], [[motifs/death\|death]]’s counterfeit,<br>And look on [[motifs/death\|death]] itself. Up, up, and see<br>The great doom’s image. [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]], [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]],<br>As from your graves rise up, and walk like sprites<br>To countenance this horror.<br>Bell rings. Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | What’s the business,<br>That such a hideous trumpet calls to parley<br>The sleepers of the house? Speak, speak. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | O gentle lady,<br>’Tis not for you to hear what I can speak.<br>The repetition in a woman’s ear<br>Would murder as it fell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | O [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]],<br>Our royal master’s murdered! |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Woe,<br>alas—<br>What, in our house? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Too cruel anywhere.<br>Dear Duff, I prithee contradict thyself,<br>And say it is not so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], Lennox, [and Ross] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Had I but died an hour before this chance<br>I had lived a blessed [[motifs/time\|time]], for from this instant<br>There’s nothing serious in [[concepts/mortality\|mortality]].<br>All is but toys. Renown and grace is dead.<br>The wine of life is drawn, and the mere lees<br>Is left this vault to brag of. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]] and Donalbain |
| DONALBAIN | What is amiss? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | You are, and do not know’t.<br>The [[motifs/spring\|spring]], the head, the fountain of your [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>Is stopped, the very source of it is stopped. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Your royal father’s murdered. |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | O, by whom? |
| LENNOX | Those of his chamber, as it seemed, had done’t.<br>Their hands and faces were all badged with [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>So were their daggers, which, unwiped, we found<br>Upon their pillows. They stared and were distracted.<br>No man’s life was to be trusted with them. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | O, yet I do repent me of my fury<br>That I did kill them. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Wherefore did you so? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Who can be wise, amazed, temp‘rate and furious,<br>Loyal and neutral in a moment? No man.<br>Th’expedition of my violent [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>Outran the pauser, reason. Here lay [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]],<br>His silver skin laced with his golden [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>And his gashed stabs looked like a breach in [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>For ruin’s wasteful entrance; there the murderers,<br>Steeped in the colours of their trade, their daggers<br>Unmannerly breeched with gore. Who could refrain,<br>That had a heart to [[motifs/love\|love]], and in that heart<br>[[concepts/courage\|Courage]] to make ’s [[motifs/love\|love]] known? |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Help me hence, ho! |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Look to the lady. |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | (aside to Donalbain)<br>Why do we hold our tongues,<br>That most may claim this argument for ours? |
| DONALBAIN | (aside to [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]])<br>What should be spoken here, where our fate,<br>Hid in an auger-hole, may rush and seize us?<br>Let’s away. Our [[motifs/tears\|tears]] are not yet brewed. |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | (aside to Donalbain)<br>Nor our strong sorrow<br>Upon the foot of motion. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Look to the lady; Exit [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]], attended<br>And when we have our naked frailties hid,<br>That suffer in exposure, let us meet<br>And question this most bloody piece of work,<br>To know it further. Fears and scruples shake us.<br>In the great hand of God I stand, and thence<br>Against the undivulged pretence I fight<br>Of treasonous malice. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | And SO do I. |
| SO | all. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Let’s briefly put on manly readiness,<br>And meet i’th’ hall together. |
| ALL | [[motifs/well\|Well]] contented. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]] and Donalbain |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | What will you do? Let’s not consort with them.<br>To show an unfelt sorrow is an office<br>Which the false man does easy. I’ll to England. |
| DONALBAIN | To Ireland, I. Our separated fortune<br>Shall keep us both the safer. Where we are<br>There’s daggers in men’s smiles. The nea’er in [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>The nearer bloody. |
| [[characters/malcolm\|MALCOLM]] | This murderous shaft that’s shot<br>Hath not yet lighted, and our safest way<br>Is to avoid the aim. Therefore to [[motifs/horse\|horse]],<br>And let us not be dainty of leave-taking,<br>But shift away. There’s warrant in that theft<br>Which steals itself when there’s no [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] left. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Ross with an Old Man |
| OLD MAN | Threescore and ten I can remember [[motifs/well\|well]], |
| *(didascalia)* | Within the volume of which [[motifs/time\|time]] I have seen |
| OLD MAN | Hours dreadful and things strange, but this sore [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>Hath trifled former knowings.<br>Ross Ha,<br>good father,<br>Thou seest the heavens, as troubled with man’s act,<br>Threatens his bloody stage. By th’ [[motifs/clock\|clock]] ’tis day,<br>And yet dark [[motifs/night\|night]] strangles the travelling lamp.<br>Is’t [[motifs/night\|night]]’s predominance or the day’s shame<br>That darkness does the face of earth entomb<br>When living light should kiss it? |
| OLD MAN | ’Tis unnatural,<br>Even like the deed that’s done. On Tuesday last<br>A falcon, tow’ring in her [[concepts/pride\|pride]] of place,<br>Was by a mousing owl hawked at and killed. |
| ROSS | And [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]’s horses—a thing most strange and<br>certain—<br>Beauteous and swift, the minions of their race,<br>Turned wild in [[concepts/nature\|nature]], broke their stalls, flung out,<br>Contending ’gainst obedience, as they would<br>Make war with mankind. |
| OLD MAN | ’Tis said they ate each other. |
| ROSS | They did so, to th’amazement of mine eyes<br>That looked upon’t. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]] |
| ROSS | Here comes the good [[characters/macduff\|Macduff]].<br>How goes the world, sir, now? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Why, see you not? |
| ROSS | Is’t known who did this more than bloody deed? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Those that [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] hath slain.<br>Ross<br>Alas the day,<br>What good could they pretend? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | They were suborned.<br>[[characters/malcolm\|Malcolm]] and Donalbain, the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s two sons,<br>Are stol’n away and fled, which puts upon them<br>Suspicion of the deed.<br>Ross<br>’Gainst [[concepts/nature\|nature]] still.<br>Thriftless [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]], that will raven up<br>Thine own life’s means! Then ’tis most like<br>The sovereignty will fall upon [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | He is already named and gone to Scone<br>To be invested.<br>Ross Where is [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]]’s [[concepts/body\|body]]? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | Carried to Colmekill,<br>The sacred storehouse of his predecessors,<br>And guardian of their bones.<br>Ross<br>Will you to Scone? |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | No, cousin, I’ll to Fife. |
| ROSS | [[motifs/well\|Well]], I will thither. |
| [[characters/macduff\|MACDUFF]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], may you see things [[motifs/well\|well]] done there. Adieu,<br>Lest our old robes sit easier than our new.<br>Ross Farewell, father. |
| OLD MAN | God’s benison go with you, and with those<br>That would make good of bad, and friends of foes. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Thou hast it now: [[archetypes/king\|King]], Cawdor, Glamis, all<br>As the weird women promised; and I [[concepts/fear\|fear]]<br>Thou played’st most foully for’t. Yet it was said<br>It should not stand in thy posterity,<br>But that myself should be the root and father<br>Of many kings. If there come [[concepts/truth\|truth]] from them—<br>As upon thee, [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]], their speeches shine-<br>Why by the verities on thee made good<br>May they not be my oracles as [[motifs/well\|well]],<br>And set me up in [[concepts/hope\|hope]]? But hush, no more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sennet sounded. Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] as [[archetypes/king\|King]], [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | as [[characters/queen\|Queen]], Lennox, Ross, lords, and attendants |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Here’s our chief guest. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | If he had been forgotten<br>It had been as a gap in our great feast,<br>And all-thing unbecoming. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (to [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]])<br>Tonight we hold a solemn supper, sir,<br>And I’ll request your presence. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Let your highness<br>Command upon me, to the which my duties<br>Are with a most indissoluble tie<br>For ever knit. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Ride you this afternoon? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | We should have else desired your good advice,<br>Which still hath been both [[motifs/grave\|grave]] and prosperous,<br>In this day’s council; but we’ll talk tomorrow.<br>Is’t far you ride? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | As far, my lord, as will fill up the [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>’Twixt this and supper. Go not my [[motifs/horse\|horse]] the better,<br>I must become a borrower of the [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>For a dark hour or twain. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Fail not our feast. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | My lord, I will not. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | We hear our bloody cousins are bestowed<br>In England and in Ireland, not confessing<br>Their cruel parricide, filling their hearers<br>With strange invention. But of that tomorrow,<br>When therewithal we shall have cause of state<br>Craving us jointly. Hie you to [[motifs/horse\|horse]]. Adieu,<br>Till you return at [[motifs/night\|night]]. Goes Fleance with you? |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | Ay, my good lord. Our [[motifs/time\|time]] does call upon ’s. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I wish your horses swift and sure of foot,<br>And so I do commend you to their backs.<br>Farewell. Exit [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]<br>Let every man be master of his [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Till seven at [[motifs/night\|night]]. To make society<br>The sweeter welcome, we will keep ourself<br>Till supper-[[motifs/time\|time]] alone. While then, God be with you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] and a Servant |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Sirrah, a word with you. Attend those men<br>Our pleasure? |
| SERVANT | They are, my lord, without the palace gate. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Bring them before us. Exit Servant<br>To be thus is nothing<br>But to be safely thus. Our fears in [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]<br>Stick deep, and in his royalty of [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Reigns that which would be feared. ‘Tis much he dares,<br>And to that dauntless temper of his mind<br>He hath a wisdom that doth guide his valour<br>To act in safety. There is none but he<br>Whose being I do [[concepts/fear\|fear]], and under him<br>My genius is rebuked as, it is said,<br>[[characters/mark-antony\|Mark Antony]]’s was by Caesar. He chid the sisters<br>When first they put the name of [[archetypes/king\|king]] upon me,<br>And bade them speak to him. Then, [[archetypes/prophet\|prophet]]-like,<br>They hailed him father to a line of kings.<br>Upon my head they placed a fruitless [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>And put a barren sceptre in my grip,<br>Thence to be wrenched with an unlineal hand,<br>No son of mine succeeding. If’t be so,<br>For [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]’s issue have I filed my mind,<br>For them the gracious [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]] have I murdered,<br>Put rancours in the vessel of my peace<br>Only for them, and mine eternal [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]]<br>Given to the common enemy of man<br>To make them kings, the seeds of [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] kings.<br>Rather than so, come fate into the list<br>And champion me to th’utterance. Who’s there? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Servant and two Murderers |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (To the Servant) Now go to the door, and stay there till<br>we call. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Servant |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Was it not yesterday we spoke together? |
| MURDERERS | It was, so please your highness. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]] then, now<br>Have you considered of my speeches? Know<br>That it was he in the times past which held you<br>So under fortune, which you thought had been<br>Our [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] [[concepts/self\|self]]. This I made good to you<br>In our last conference, passed in probation with you<br>How you were borne in hand, how crossed, the<br>instruments,<br>Who wrought with them, and all things else that<br>might<br>To half a [[motifs/soul\|soul]], and to a notion crazed,<br>Say ‘Thus did [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]’. |
| FIRST MURDERER | You made it known to us. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I did so, and went further, which is now<br>Our point of second meeting. Do you find<br>Your patience so predominant in your [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>That you can let this go? Are you so gospelled<br>To pray for this good man and for his issue,<br>Whose heavy hand hath bowed you to the [[motifs/grave\|grave]]<br>And beggared yours for ever? |
| FIRST MURDERER | We are men, my liege. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Ay, in the catalogue ye go for men,<br>As hounds and greyhounds, mongrels, spaniels, curs,<br>Shoughs, [[motifs/water\|water]]-rugs, and demi-wolves are clept<br>All by the name of dogs. The valued file<br>Distinguishes the swift, the slow, the subtle,<br>The housekeeper, the hunter, every one<br>According to the gift which bounteous [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Hath in him closed; whereby he does receive<br>Particular addition from the bill<br>That writes them all alike. And so of men.<br>Now, if you have a station in the file,<br>Not i‘th’ worst rank of manhood, say’t,<br>And I will put that business in your bosoms<br>Whose execution takes your enemy off,<br>Grapples you to the heart and [[motifs/love\|love]] of us,<br>Who wear our health but sickly in his life,<br>Which in his [[motifs/death\|death]] were perfect. |
| SECOND MURDERER | I am one, my liege,<br>Whom the vile blows and buffets of the world<br>Hath so incensed that I am reckless what<br>I do to spite the world. |
| FIRST MURDERER | And I another,<br>So weary with disasters, tugged with fortune,<br>That I would set my life on any chance<br>To mend it or be rid on’t. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Both of you<br>Know [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] was your enemy. |
| MURDERERS | True, my lord. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | So is he mine, and in such bloody distance<br>That every minute of his being thrusts<br>Against my near’st of life; and though I could<br>With barefaced power sweep him from my sight<br>And bid my will avouch it, yet I must not,<br>For certain friends that are both his and mine,<br>Whose loves I may not drop, but wail his fall<br>Who I myself struck down. And thence it is<br>That I to your assistance do make [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Masking the business from the common eye<br>For sundry weighty reasons. |
| SECOND MURDERER | We shall, my lord,<br>Perform what you command us. |
| FIRST MURDERER | Though our lives— |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Your spirits shine through you. Within this hour at most<br>I will advise you where to plant yourselves,<br>Acquaint you with the perfect spy o‘th’ [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>The moment on’t; for’t must be done tonight,<br>And something from the palace; always thought<br>That I require a clearness; and with him,<br>To leave no rubs nor botches in the work,<br>Fleance, his son, that keeps him company—<br>Whose absence is no less material to me<br>Than is his father’s—must embrace the fate<br>Of that dark hour. Resolve yourselves apart.<br>I’ll come to you anon. |
| MURDERERS | We are resolved, my lord. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | I’ll call upon you straight. Abide within. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Murderers |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | It is concluded. [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], thy [[motifs/soul\|soul]]’s flight,<br>If it find heaven, must find it out tonight. Exit<br><br>3.2 Enter [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] and a Servant |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Is [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] gone from [[settings/court\|court]]? |
| SERVANT | Ay, madam, but returns again tonight. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Say to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] I would attend his leisure<br>For a few words. |
| SERVANT | Madam, I will. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Naught’s had, all’s spent,<br>Where our [[motifs/desire\|desire]] is got without content.<br>’Tis safer to be that which we destroy<br>Than by destruction dwell in doubtful joy. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | How now, my lord, why do you keep alone,<br>Of sorriest fancies your companions making,<br>Using those thoughts which should indeed have died<br>With them they think on? Things without all remedy<br>Should be without regard. What’s done is done. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | We have scorched the snake, not killed it.<br>She’ll close and be herself, whilst our poor malice<br>Remains in danger of her former tooth.<br>But let the frame of things disjoint, both the worlds<br>suffer,<br>Ere we will eat our meal in [[concepts/fear\|fear]], and [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]<br>In the affliction of these terrible [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]]<br>That shake us nightly. Better be with the dead,<br>Whom we to gain our peace have sent to peace,<br>Than on the torture of the mind to lie<br>In restless ecstasy. [[characters/duncan\|Duncan]] is in his [[motifs/grave\|grave]].<br>After life’s fitful fever he sleeps [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>Treason has done his worst. Nor steel nor [[motifs/poison\|poison]],<br>Malice domestic, foreign levy, nothing<br>Can touch him further. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Come on, gentle my lord,<br>Sleek o’er your rugged looks, be bright and jovial<br>Among your guests tonight. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | So shall I, [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>And so I pray be you. Let your remembrance<br>Apply to [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]. Present him eminence<br>Both with eye and tongue; unsafe the while that we<br>Must lave our honours in these flattering streams<br>And make our faces visors to our hearts,<br>Disguising what they are. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | You must leave this. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | O, full of scorpions is my mind, dear wife!<br>Thou know’st that [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and his Fleance lives. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | But in them [[concepts/nature\|nature]]’s copy’s not eterne. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | There’s comfort yet, they are assailable.<br>Then be thou jocund. Ere the bat hath flown<br>His cloistered flight, ere to black Hecate’s summons<br>The shard-borne beetle with his drowsy hums<br>Hath rung [[motifs/night\|night]]’s yawning peal, there shall be done<br>A deed of dreadful note. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | What’s to be done? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Be [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] of the knowledge, dearest chuck,<br>Till thou applaud the deed.—Come, seeling [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>Scarf up the tender eye of pitiful day,<br>And with thy bloody and invisible hand<br>Cancel and tear to pieces that great bond<br>Which keeps me pale. Light thickens, and the crow<br>Makes wing to th’ rooky wood.<br>Good things of day begin to droop and drowse,<br>Whiles [[motifs/night\|night]]’s black agents to their preys do rouse.<br>Thou marvell’st at my words; but hold thee still.<br>Things bad begun make strong themselves by ill.<br>So prithee go with me. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter three Murderers |
| FIRST MURDERER | (to Third Murderer)<br>But who did bid thee join with us? |
| THIRD MURDERER | [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]. |
| SECOND MURDERER | (to First Murderer)<br>He needs not our mistrust, since he delivers<br>Our offices and what we have to do<br>To the direction just. |
| FIRST MURDERER | (to Third Murderer) Then stand with us.<br>The west yet glimmers with some streaks of day.<br>Now spurs the lated traveller apace<br>To gain the timely inn, and near approaches<br>The subject of our watch. |
| THIRD MURDERER | Hark, I hear horses. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | (within)<br>Give us a light there, ho! |
| SECOND MURDERER | Then ’tis he. The rest<br>That are within the note of expectation<br>Already are i’th’ [[settings/court\|court]]. |
| FIRST MURDERER | His horses go about. |
| THIRD MURDERER | Almost a mile; but he does usually,<br>So all men do, from hence to th’ palace gate<br>Make it their walk. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] and Fleance with a torch |
| SECOND MURDERER | (aside) A light, a light. |
| THIRD MURDERER | (aside)<br>’Tis he. |
| FIRST MURDERER | (aside) Stand to’t. |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | It will be rain tonight. |
| FIRST MURDERER | Let it come down.<br>First Murderer strikes out the torch. The others attack [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] |
| [[characters/banquo\|BANQUO]] | O, treachery! Fly, good Fleance, fly, fly, fly!<br>Thou mayst [[concepts/revenge\|revenge]].—O slave! He dies. Exit Fleance |
| THIRD MURDERER | Who did strike out the light? |
| FIRST MURDERER | Was’t not the way? |
| THIRD MURDERER | There’s but one down. The son is fled. |
| SECOND MURDERER | We have lost best half of our affair. |
| FIRST MURDERER | [[motifs/well\|Well]], let’s away and say how much is done. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]’s [[concepts/body\|body]] |

### Atto 3, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Banquet prepared. Enter [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] as [[archetypes/king\|King]], [[characters/lady-macbeth\|Lady Macbeth]] as [[characters/queen\|Queen]], Ross, Lennox, Lords, and attendants. Lady |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]] sits |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | You know your own degrees; sit down. At first and last<br>The hearty welcome. |
| LORDS | Thanks to your majesty.<br>They sit |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Ourself will mingle with society<br>And play the humble host. Our hostess keeps her<br>state,<br>But in best [[motifs/time\|time]] we will require her welcome. |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | Pronounce it for me, sir, to all our friends,<br>For my heart speaks they are welcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter First Murderer [to the door] |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | See, they encounter thee with their hearts’ thanks.<br>Both sides are even. Here I’ll sit, i’th’ midst.<br>Be large in mirth. Anon we’ll drink a measure<br>The table round. (To First Murderer) There’s [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>upon thy face. |
| FIRST MURDERER | (aside to [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]) ‘Tis [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]’s, then. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | ’Tis better thee without than he within.<br>Is he dispatched? |
| FIRST MURDERER | My lord, his throat is cut. That I did for him. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Thou art the best o’th’ cut-throats. Yet he’s good<br>That did the like for Fleance. If thou didst it,<br>Thou art the nonpareil. |
| FIRST MURDERER | Most royal sir,<br>Fleance is scaped. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Then comes my fit again; I had else been perfect,<br>Whole as the marble, founded as the rock,<br>As broad and general as the casing air,<br>But now I am cabined, cribbed, confined, bound in<br>To saucy doubts and fears. But [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]]’s safe? |
| FIRST MURDERER | Ay, my good lord. Safe in a ditch he bides,<br>With twenty trenched gashes on his head,<br>The least a [[motifs/death\|death]] to [[concepts/nature\|nature]]. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Thanks for that.<br>There the grown serpent lies. The worm that’s fled<br>Hath [[concepts/nature\|nature]] that in [[motifs/time\|time]] will venom breed,<br>No teeth for th’ present. Get thee gone. Tomorrow<br>We’ll hear ourselves again. Exit First Murderer |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | My royal lord,<br>You do not give the cheer. The feast is sold<br>That is not often vouched, while ‘tis a-making,<br>’Tis given with welcome. To feed were best at [[motifs/home\|home]].<br>From thence the sauce to meat is ceremony,<br>Meeting were bare without it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[archetypes/ghost\|Ghost]] of [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]], and sits in [[characters/macbeth\|Macbeth]]’s |
| [[characters/lady-macbeth\|LADY MACBETH]] | place |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Sweet remembrancer.<br>Now good digestion wait on appetite,<br>And health on both. |
| LENNOX | May’t please your highness sit? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Here had we now our country’s [[concepts/honour\|honour]] roofed<br>Were the graced person of our [[characters/banquo\|Banquo]] present,<br>Who may I rather challenge for unkindness<br>Than pity for mischance. |
| ROSS | His absence, sir,<br>Lays blame upon his promise. Please’t your highness<br>To grace us with your royal company? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | The table’s full. |
| LENNOX | Here is a place reserved, sir. |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Where? |
| LENNOX | Here, my good lord. What is’t that moves your<br>highness? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | Which of you have done this? |
| LORDS | What, my good lord? |
| [[characters/macbeth\|MACBETH]] | (to the [[archetypes/ghost\|Ghost]])<br>Thou canst not say I did it. Never shake<br>Thy gory locks at me. |
| ROSS | (rising)<br>Gentlemen, rise. His highness is not [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
